    "evidence_text": "Appl. Sci. 2021 ,11, 5359 12 of 17\nstructure to break up at temperatures between 50 and 75◦C, resulting in the intermolecular\nforces collapsing and the storage modulus signiﬁcantly decreasing. This result is presented\nin Figure 8b, indicating that the Tan δof TPU-PTC-BDO increased dramatically between 50\nand 75◦C. In a glassy state, the TPU-PTC copolymers possess more carbonate groups so\nthat the polymer chains are restricted in mobility, contrary to the TPU-PHC copolymers\nthat own the long carbon chains [50].\n3.6. Tensile Strength\nThe molecular weight of a polymer is a key factor affecting its tensile strength. As\nmentioned in Section 3.3, the TPU copolymers using the same chain extender had com-\nparable molecular weights. Thus, the molecular weight can be ignored in the analysis\nof tensile strength. As Table 6 indicates, the TPU had high elongations at break (above\n460%), and both the TPU-PHC-PDO and TPU-PHC-BDO had moderate strain at break.\nHowever, the TPU-PHC-BDO and TPU-PTC-PDO differed considerably in terms of the\nstrain at break. The results are possibly affected by hydrogen bonding. According to\nthe FT-IR results in Section 3.1 (Scheme 1a), the TPU-PHC-BDO and TPU-PHC-PDO had\nstrong intermolecular hydrogen bonds in the TPU-PTC-BDO; because the regularity of the\nmolecular chains was poor (atactic) and the intermolecular attraction weak, poor strength\nresulted. Notably, TPU-PTC-PDO uses an odd-numbered polyol and chain extender to\ncause regular molecular chains (isotactic), stacking well, and more secondary bonds (such\nas hydrogen bonds, benzene π−πstacking, and dipole–dipole interactions) (Scheme 1b).\nBoth of the copolymers had the same number of the hydrogen bonds per unit in Scheme\n1b, and whereas we can see that Scheme 1b focuses on the blue and red squares in the\nsoft segments, TPU-PTC-PDO could have possible dipole–dipole force ( δ+toδ−) and\nTPU-PTC-BDO does not ( δ−toδ−). More dipole interactions for TPU copolymers can drive\nbetter mechanical strength. According to the literature, the storage modulus increases with\ndipole interactions under a glassy state [ 51]. We also saw this phenomenon in this study,\nand Figure 8a in the manuscript revealed that the storage modulus of TPU-PTC-PDO was\nhigher than TPU-PTC-BDO., It can be compared to Scheme 1b to prove that TPU-PTC-PDO\nhas more dipole interactions than TPU-PTC-BDO. Thus, it has a high tensile strength\nclose to TPU-PHC-BDO and TPU-PHC-PDO (Table 6). The regularity of molecular chains\nalso affected the stretching process. As is shown in Figure 9a,b, the stress of the TPU\nsamples presented a rapid rise at approximately 300% of elongation, except in the case of\nTPU-PTC-BDO. This was because TPU-PTC-BDO is an odd-numbered carbon chain (a long\nchain of three carbons) corresponding to an even-numbered carbon chain (a short chain\nof four carbons), and it cannot be arranged regularly after stress as is expected in strain-\ninduced crystallization [ 52–55]. Therefore, the intermolecular hydrogen bonding effect for\nthe TPU-PTC-BDO was lower than for other samples. Accordingly, the TPU-PTC-BDO\npresented the lowest stress but the highest elongation.\nTable 6.
